{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YZfz3fxbBQY2TpAJzhRehF","type":"tool_result","content":"# Call onError for a rejection that is not an Error\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#95\nLabels: priority:medium, type:bug, size:S, area:api\n\n## Why\n\nThe orchestrator's `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error`. A rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string, a plain object, a value from a library that rejects with its own shape — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.\n\n`onError` is the seam a deployer uses to count failures, page someone, or write an incident record. A gate that silently drops a class of failures makes it a liar in exactly the cases nobody tested for, and the deployer's dashboard reads \"no errors\" while the caller sees rejections.\n\nRecorded as measured behaviour in `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` by HALEngine#83, which scoped itself to specifying the seam rather than changing it, and raised again by the reviewer on that PR. The spec notes this property is owned by no issue in the epic; this is that issue.\n\n## What to do\n\nCall `onError` for every rejection. Whatever is not already an `Error` gets wrapped in one, preserving the original as the `cause`, so a hook typed against `Error` keeps compiling and a hook that wants the raw value can still reach it. The re-thrown value at the caller boundary stays exactly what it is today, so nothing downstream changes shape.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] A provider rejecting with a string calls `onError` once, with an `Error` whose `cause` is that string, and the caller still receives the original rejection unchanged\n- [ ] The same holds for a rejection with a plain object and with `undefined`\n- [ ] A rejection that is already an `Error` reaches `onError` as the same instance, not a wrapper, pinned by the existing tests remaining unedited\n- [ ] `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` replaces its recorded-flaw line with the new guarantee, and the CHANGELOG says what changes for an existing consumer\n- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`) and `npm run prettier:check` pass\n\n## Tech notes\n\nKey files: `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` (the `catch` and its `instanceof Error` gate), `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md`.\n\nOut of scope: `beforeSession` escaping the `try` (its own issue) and usage accounting across tool rounds (HALEngine#93).\n\n---\n## CI reported failures on f37b91d3241816f6a69bd0bc19f8b2735a12cca3\n\nThe build for your last push is red. These checks failed: eslint, spec-names, changelog\n\nWhere a failed step is named below, run only that step's command; otherwise map each name to the job that publishes it and run only that job's describing the behaviour change for existing consumers\n---\nOn branch loop-bench/95\nYour branch is up to date with 'origin/loop-bench/95'.\n\nnothing to commit, working tree clean\nloop-bench/95\n# Definition of Done\n\n> The orchestrator's `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error`. A rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string, a plain object, a response body rejected directly by an HTTP client — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.\n\n**Strategy: `direct`** — the seam already exists. `createChatOrchestrator` returns an orchestrator whose `processMessage` and `processMessageStream` invoke the installed `OrchestratorHooks`. A test can drive `processMessage` with a provider that throws a string or a plain object and observe that `onError` is bypassed today because of the `error instanceof Error` gate in the `catch` block.\n\n## Done when these pass\n\n- [ ] **calls onError with an Error carrying the thrown string and runs afterSession while rethrowing** — when a turn rejects with a string (`'string boom'`), `onError` is called once with an `Error` instance whose `message` is `'string boom'`, `afterSession` runs after `onError`, and the rejection still propagates to the caller. Fails today because `onError` is skipped (`callOrder` is `['afterSession']` and `isError` is `false`).\n      `src/orchestration/onErrorNonError.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **calls onError with an Error carrying a readable message when the stream rejects with a plain object** — when a turn rejects with a plain object (`{code: 503, detail: 'upstream down'}`), `onError` is called once with an `Error` instance carrying a non-empty `message`, `afterSession` runs after `onError`, and the rejection still propagates to the caller. Fails today because `onError` is skipped (`callOrder` is `['afterSession']` and `isError` is `false`).\n      `src/orchestration/onErrorNonError.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [x] In `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts`, coerce a caught non-`Error` rejection to an `Error` with a readable `message` (while passing an `Error` instance through unchanged) so `hooks.onError` always fires on any rejection inside the `try` block.\n- [x] Confirm existing `Error`-instance tests in `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts` (`called with session and error when stream fails`, `error still propagates after onError hook`, `error path calls onError then afterSession`) remain green without modification.\n- [x] Make the message of a non-string, non-Error rejection readable (a plain object currently becomes `[object Object]`, `undefined` becomes `undefined`) while keeping `cause` the original value.\n- [x] Add a `CHANGELOG.md` entry under `## [Unreleased]` describing the behaviour change for existing consumers.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- `beforeSession` escaping the `try` block (resolved separately in HALEngine#94).\n- Usage accounting across tool rounds (resolved separately in HALEngine#93).\n- Widening the `onError` hook parameter type away from `Error` (the ticket keeps `(session: ChatSession, error: Error) => Promise<void>`).","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d0b47252-f84a-4d84-a3ea-d6613060a07e","uuid":"528757bf-5d38-493a-8305-61d6853f5d73","timestamp":"2026-11-02T16:24:08.930Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"# Call onError for a rejection that is not an Error\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#95\nLabels: priority:medium, type:bug, size:S, area:api\n\n## Why\n\nThe orchestrator's `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error`. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01GQikSYvHHdPRst2RcHo8KA","type":"tool_result","content":"b587604 docs(orchestrator): correct onError changelog wording\n6d3fcd9 fix(orchestrator): drop formatter churn from architecture spec\n92b378b fix(orchestrator): repoint spec links and changelog for onError\nf37b91d fix(orchestrator): give non-Error rejections a readable message\na66367c fix(orchestrator): call onError for non-Error rejections\n64143f7 test(orchestrator): pin onError on non-Error rejection\n684b294 Merge pull request #118 from re-cinq/dependabot/npm_and_yarn/production-4ba7a28995\ne8d1bfb Merge pull request #97 from re-cinq/lore/implementation-loop/issue-39\nFrom /var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/e0e956158887/origin\n * branch            main       -> FETCH_HEAD\n684b294 Merge pull request #118 from re-cinq/dependabot/npm_and_yarn/production-4ba7a28995\ne8d1bfb Merge pull request #97 from re-cinq/lore/implementation-loop/issue-39\n24771d1 merge main into issue-39 and close the two open acceptance criteria\nAGENTS.md\nCHANGELOG.md\nCLAUDE.md\nCODE_OF_CONDUCT.md\nCONTRIBUTING.md\nLICENSE\nREADME.md\nSECURITY.md\nadrs\ndocs\neslint.config.backlog.mjs\neslint.config.mjs\nexample\njest.config.js\nlayers.yaml\nnode_modules\npackage-lock.json\npackage.json\nscripts\nsmoke\nspecs\nsrc\ntsconfig.build.json\ntsconfig.example.json\ntsconfig.json\n# Lore test-command interface (lore_list_tests / lore_run_test).\n# Written for this repo's Jest suite rather than adapted from the Vitest\n# equivalent for a Vitest suite: the report shapes happen to match, but the\n# invocations do not.\n- id: hal-engine-jest\n  name: HAL Engine Jest suite\n  cwd: .\n  description: >-\n    The ts-jest unit suite over src/ and scripts/ (jest.config.js roots), run\n    on every pull request in .github/workflows/ci.yml as the \"Test\" step.\n    Selectors are \"<repo-relative-file>::<full test name>\" as emitted by the\n    list command. Jest has no dry-run, so the list command executes the suite\n    once with --json and derives every name from the report; it deliberately\n    tolerates a red suite, since listing must not depend on the tests passing.\n    The run command selects one test with --runTestsByPath plus an anchored,\n    regex-escaped -t, and treats a selector that matches nothing as a failure\n    rather than a green run of an empty suite. Neither command builds first:\n    no test here reads dist/.\n  list: node scripts/lore-list-tests.mjs\n  run: node scripts/lore-run-test.mjs \"{selector}\"\n  coverage_format: lcov\n# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to `@re-cinq/hal-engine` are documented here.\n\nThe format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). Entries are written for somebody installing the\npackage, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n### Added\n\n- `SessionStore` gains an optional `save(session)` member: the write signal a durable store needs. The engine calls it once per processed user message, from the same `finally` path that runs `afterSession`, so it fires for a turn that ended in a provider error as well as one that succeeded — a store that only heard about successes would lose exactly the conversations a customer complains about. By the time it runs, the turn's entries are committed, so the assistant entry's `isStreaming` is already `false` — including when a provider threw mid-stream, where the engine now closes the open entry before saving, recording `truncated` on it, rather than persisting a half-streamed entry that looks finished. An `afterSession` hook that throws no longer costs the turn its write signal. What `save` throws or rejects with is caught and logged as one line carrying the session id, and the turn continues: the client still receives its full stream and `stream_end`, because a database outage is not a reason for the agent to stop answering. The member is typed `void | Promise<void>`, so a synchronous implementation and an `async` one both satisfy it. Additive: `InMemorySessionStore` does not implement `save` and is unchanged, and a store that omits it behaves exactly as before.\n- `SessionStore` gains an optional `evict(sessionId)` member, and `InMemorySessionStore` implements it. The engine calls it only for a session whose socket closed while `create` was still pending — one that no client ever received and that has no entries to keep. `InMemorySessionStore` now also takes `{maxAgeMs}` (default `28_800_000`, eight hours), exported as `InMemorySessionStoreOptions`. Expired sessions are evicted when read and swept at the head of `create`, which stops at the first live session, so the bound costs nothing on a busy store. There is no timer: a library-owned interval would keep your process alive.\n- **`MongoSessionStore`**, a durable session store, exported as `MongoSessionStore` and `createMongoSessionStore`. It takes a collection, a client with a database name, or a connection URL — nothing in the package reads the environment or assumes a host. The collection is the truth and the in-process cache is an optimisation: a session written by `save` is readable by a second store on the same collection. `delete` removes the document — it is the consumer's erasure primitive and the engine never calls it — as do `eraseConversation`, `eraseOlderThan(cutoff)` and `eraseAll`, while `clear` alone touches only the cache; `docs/session-stores.md` opens with the table that says which is which, because the previous documentation would have led you to believe `delete` erased. Documents carry `createdAt` and `updatedAt`, so a retention period is expressible without per-entry timestamps. **No credential is ever written**: `authHeaders`, `authorization`, `cookie` and `host` are stripped at any depth, not just the top level, and the same `stripCredentialKeys` is exported for stores you write yourself. `mongodb` is an optional peer dependency loaded only when the store actually connects, so a consumer who installs no optional peer can still import the package root and use the in-memory store. **Migration note**: `delete` and `evict` both erase on this store, so the one-line `onDisconnect: sessionId => store.delete(sessionId)` that restores the pre-0.4 close behaviour destroys the conversation on every socket close — point `onDisconnect` at neither. Nothing needs reclaiming by hand: the in-process cache is bounded by `maxAgeMs` (eight hours by default) and drops stale entries without touching a document. This does **not** deliver conversation resume, and it does not make the engine safe to run as more than one instance against one conversation.\n- `withRetry(provider, policy)` decorator wraps any `AIProvider` with per-attempt retry and timeout logic. Pass it a `RetryPolicy` — all fields optional — to absorb transient provider failures before they reach the caller. Defaults: `maxAttempts: 3`, `baseDelayMs: 500`, `maxDelayMs: 5000`, `firstChunkTimeoutMs: 30000`, `idleChunkTimeoutMs: 30000`. A hung request (first chunk never arrives) is abandoned and retried; a stalled stream (idle after the first chunk) surfaces as an `AIError` with code `TIMEOUT` and is not retried, because chunks already streamed cannot be un-sent. Backoff is exponential with full jitter. Both `withRetry` and `RetryPolicy` are exported from the package root.\n- `HalEngineConfig` gains an optional `resilience?: RetryPolicy` field. When present, `createHalEngine` wraps the provider with `withRetry` between `createProvider` and `createChatOrchestrator`; when absent, behaviour is unchanged. This is a strictly additive change — no existing call to `createHalEngine` is affected, so the version bump is MINOR.\n\n### Changed\n\n- Every `SessionStore` member is now `Awaitable<T>` (`T | Promise<T>`) rather than a bare `T`, so a store backed by a database is expressible for the first time. `Awaitable` is exported from the package root. **A synchronous implementation needs no change** — `T` is assignable to `T | Promise<T>` in a return position — and `InMemorySessionStore` is unedited. The WebSocket connection handler now awaits `create` and the release that follows a close: it pauses the socket across the await so a frame sent before `connected` arrives is buffered rather than dropped, attaches its `close` listener before the await so a socket that closes mid-create leaves no session behind, and catches a rejection from either call so one store failure closes one socket instead of ending the process with an unhandled rejection. **Migration note**: the `connected` frame now arrives one tick later, after the awaited `create`, so a client that waits for it (as the protocol already requires) sees no difference; only code that assumed the frame was emitted synchronously with the upgrade does.\n- **The engine no longer erases a conversation when its socket closes.** The WebSocket close handler called `sessionStore.delete(sessionId)` unconditionally, which meant that behind a durable store every conversation was written and then destroyed the moment the customer closed their tab — the opposite of what this engine's own documentation promised. `SessionStore.delete` now has no engine-internal caller at all and is purely your erasure primitive. The credentials the socket carried are cleared from the session on close, since they were issued for a request that is over; the entries are untouched. **Migration note**: to restore the old behaviour exactly, pass `onDisconnect: sessionId => store.delete(sessionId)` — that one line is the whole migration. If you rely on the default store not growing, note that it is now bounded by `maxAgeMs` (eight hours) rather than by the close handler; that is a memory bound, not a retention policy, and the retention decision remains yours.\n- The `onError` hook now fires for every rejection inside a turn, not only those that are an `Error`. A provider that rejects with a string or a plain object used to skip `onError` entirely while still rejecting to the caller, so a failure counter or alert built on `onError` missed those turns. Such a rejection now reaches `onError` as an `Error` whose `message` describes the thrown value and whose `cause` is the original value; an `Error` is passed through as the same instance. The caller still receives the original rejection unchanged. Existing `onError` handlers keep compiling; a thrown string arrives as the `message` unchanged, while a plain object arrives as its listed fields, so read `error.cause` for the raw value.\n- The `afterModelResponse` hook now receives the accumulated usage across every provider call in the turn, not only the last one. Existing consumers that use this hook for billing or audit will see larger totals for turns that called tools — this is the correct number. A turn with no tool calls is unchanged.\n\n### Fixed\n\n- `onError` and `afterSession` hooks now fire when `beforeSession` throws, matching the guarantee the hook names imply. Previously, a throw from `beforeSession` escaped the `try`/`finally` block entirely, so neither hook ran. **Migration note**: if your `afterSession` hook assumed it would only be called after a full session turn, it will now also be called when `beforeSession` itself fails — the hook can inspect the session to detect this case (no entries beyond the initial user message will have been processed).\n\n## [0.3.0] - 2026-09-29\n\n**Upgrading from 0.2.x.** Nothing is removed and no existing signature changes, but check these before you bump:\n\n- `OrchestratorHooks` gains two optional members, `beforeToolCall` and `onToolBudgetExhausted`. Code that checks the hooks exhaustively, for example `satisfies Record<keyof OrchestratorHooks, …>`, fails to type-check until it names both.\n- `ToolRegistry.execute` now validates tool input against each tool's `inputSchema`, so a tool that produced its own message for malformed input receives only valid input; the model reads the registry's validation message instead.\n- `maxToolRounds` now counts executed rounds: the default of 5 runs 5 tool rounds, not 6. Raise it by one to keep the old count.\n- Offering a bare access token as the only WebSocket subprotocol still works but is deprecated: offer `hal.v1` beside it now, because the next minor release stops accepting a bare token.\n- New dependencies: `ajv`, and `cookie` moves from `^0.7` to `^2`.\n\n### Added\n\n- `VertexConfig` gains an optional `apiEndpoint` field, forwarded verbatim to the `@google-cloud/vertexai` SDK constructor. Deployers who must keep inference in the EU multi-region can set `apiEndpoint: 'aiplatform.eu.rep.googleapis.com'`; the change is additive and the default behaviour (endpoint derived from `location`) is unchanged.\n- `HalEngineConfig.transport` now accepts three optional extension points: `additionalRoutes` (mounts a router under `basePath`, unauthenticated), `rootRoutes` (mounts a router at `/`, after the `basePath` router and before the 404 catch-all), and `errorHandler` (replaces Express's default HTML error page). Pass none of them and the app is identical to before. See `docs/getting-started.md` for usage and the GDPR / NIS-2 notes that apply to unauthenticated routes.\n- `OrchestratorHooks` gains an optional `beforeToolCall(session, call)` hook that fires before each known tool's executor, once per call per round. Return `undefined` to let the call run, or a `ToolResponse` to decline it: the executor is skipped and the model reads your `result` as that call's `tool_result`, with `clientMessages` and `suppressAssistantResponse` behaving as a tool's would. A hook that throws declines the call instead of failing the turn. Use it for an audit of what the model attempted or an EU AI Act Article 14 human-oversight control. The hook receives the model's raw tool input and, through `session`, the caller's `authHeaders`, so don't log either. Additive: with no hook installed, nothing changes.\n- `OrchestratorHooks` gains an optional `onToolBudgetExhausted(session, budget)` hook, and `ToolBudgetInfo` (`{maxToolRounds, requestedTools}`) and `TOOL_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` are exported. It fires when the model asks for a tool round `maxToolRounds` refuses, after the last provider call and before `afterModelResponse`. Return a sentence and the turn ends on it as an ordinary assistant entry, so the user sees a degraded answer instead of silence; the engine writes no prose of its own. Every exhausted run now also ends with a `{type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_budget_exhausted'}` chunk, a new value for the existing field rather than a new type, which the WebSocket transport sends nothing for. The turn still ends with `stream_end`, never `error`. Additive: with no hook installed, the wire is unchanged.\n- `AssistantEntry` and `ThinkingEntry` gain an optional `truncated: true`, set on an entry the run could not finish: a provider failure mid-stream, or a round that ends on a tool call with no `stop`. The server re-sends such an entry as an `entry_upsert` carrying the flag just before its `entry_commit`, and the flag stays on the stored entry, so a client can render a cut-off answer as cut off live or on a replay, without relying on the transient `error` frame. An entry committed normally never carries it; a suppressed entry is flagged in the session but never re-sent. Text the thinking-tag parser was still holding back is now kept in a cut-off entry, as it already was on a normal stop. Additive: a client that ignores the field renders what it did before, and the extra `entry_upsert` replaces the entry with the content it already has.\n\n### Changed\n\n- Every `user_message` now ends in exactly one `stream_end`, including a run that fails and a message that fails validation; the `error` frame is advisory rather than terminal. Unparseable JSON remains the one frame answered with `INVALID_FORMAT` and no `stream_end`. **Migration note**: `stream_end` may now follow an `error` frame, so treat `stream_end` (or the socket closing) as the only end of a run. The one live consumer handles the two frame types independently, and both clear its processing state, so it keeps working unchanged and no deprecation period is served by delaying.\n- Every assistant and thinking entry a run opens is now committed when the run ends, however it ends: a provider that throws mid-stream, or a round that ends on a tool call with no `stop`, no longer leaves an entry with `isStreaming: true` in the session. The partial content is kept exactly as streamed, and on a throw its `entry_commit` arrives before the `error` frame. **Migration note**: `entry_commit` now arrives on paths that previously sent none, so a client counting commit frames sees more of them. No wire type changes, and a client that already stops streaming on `stream_end` renders the same result.\n- `maxToolRounds` now bounds the tool rounds that are actually executed: at most `maxToolRounds` rounds run, and the provider is called at most `maxToolRounds + 1` times. Previously the loop ran one extra round whose tool results no provider call ever read, so the default of 5 executed 6 rounds; it now executes 5, and the sixth provider call still reads the fifth round's results. `maxToolRounds: 0` now means one provider call and no tool execution, a negative value behaves as 0, and a non-finite value falls back to the default. When the budget runs out, the engine logs one `warn` (`tool budget exhausted`) naming the tools it did not run. **Migration note**: no exported type or signature changes, but a tool with a side effect in that final, unread round no longer runs there; to restore the old count, raise `maxToolRounds` by one.\n- `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` now carries a JSDoc comment. The field already shipped\n  in `0.2.x`; the comment makes the lifecycle-hook seam discoverable from the published type\n  declarations so editors surface it on hover without a trip to the README.\n- `ToolRegistry.execute` now validates tool input against the declared `inputSchema` before invoking the executor. A schema-invalid call returns a descriptive `ToolResponse` to the model (naming the failing property path and constraint, never the value) rather than throwing, so the model can retry within the existing tool-round budget. Extra properties pass through to the executor unchanged. **Migration note**: an executor that previously hand-validated its own input now receives only input that has already passed the declared schema; a tool that relied on seeing malformed input to produce its own error message will no longer see it.\n- The WebSocket server no longer echoes the access token in its handshake response. A client offers the new `hal.v1` marker beside its token (`new WebSocket(url, ['hal.v1', token])`, or `HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL`, now exported from the package root) and the server answers only `hal.v1`, so the token stays out of the 101 response headers and off the connected socket's `protocol` property. Lore test-command interface (lore_list_tests / lore_run_test).\n# Written for this repo's Jest suite rather than adapted from the Vitest\n# equivalent for a Vitest suite: the report shapes happen to match, but the\n# invocations do not.\n- id: hal-engine-jest\n  name: HAL Engine Jest suite\n  cwd: .\n  description: >-\n    The ts-jest unit suite over src/ and scripts/ (jest.config.js roots), run\n    on every pull request in .github/workflows/ci.yml as the \"Test\" step.\n    Selectors are \"<repo-relative-file>::<full test name>\" as emitted by the\n    list command. Jest has no dry-run, so the list command executes the suite\n    once with --json and derives every name from the report; it deliberately\n    tolerates a red suite, since listing must not depend on the tests passing.\n    The run command selects one test with --runTestsByPath plus an anchored,\n    regex-escaped -t, and treats a selector that matches nothing as a failure\n    rather than a green run of an empty suite. Neither command builds first:\n    no test here reads dist/.\n  list: node scripts/lore-list-tests.mjs\n  run: node scripts/lore-run-test.mjs \"{selector}\"\n  coverage_format: lcov\n# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to `@re-cinq/hal-engine` are documented here.\n\nThe format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). Entries are written for somebody installing the\npackage, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n### Added\n\n- `SessionStore` gains an optional `save(session)` member: the write signal a durable store needs. The engine calls it once per processed user message, from the same `finally` path that runs `afterSession`, so it fires for a turn that ended in a provider error as well as one that succeeded — a store that only heard about successes would lose exactly the conversations a customer complains about. By the time it runs, the turn's entries are committed, so the assistant entry's `isStreaming` is already `false` — including when a provider threw mid-stream, where the engine now closes the open entry before saving, recording `truncated` on it, rather than persisting a half-streamed entry that looks finished. An `afterSession` hook that throws no longer costs the turn its write signal. What `save` throws or rejects with is caught and logged as one line carrying the session id, and the turn continues: the client still receives its full stream and `stream_end`, because a database outage is not a reason for the agent to stop answering. The member is typed `void | Promise<void>`, so a synchronous implementation and an `async` one both satisfy it. Additive: `InMemorySessionStore` does not implement `save` and is unchanged, and a store that omits it behaves exactly as before.\n- `SessionStore` gains an optional `evict(sessionId)` member, and `InMemorySessionStore` implements it. The engine calls it only for a session whose socket closed while `create` was still pending — one that no client ever received and that has no entries to keep. `InMemorySessionStore` now also takes `{maxAgeMs}` (default `28_800_000`, eight hours), exported as `InMemorySessionStoreOptions`. Expired sessions are evicted when read and swept at the head of `create`, which stops at the first live session, so the bound costs nothing on a busy store. There is no timer: a library-owned interval would keep your process alive.\n- **`MongoSessionStore`**, a durable session store, exported as `MongoSessionStore` and `createMongoSessionStore`. It takes a collection, a client with a database name, or a connection URL — nothing in the package reads the environment or assumes a host. The collection is the truth and the in-process cache is an optimisation: a session written by `save` is readable by a second store on the same collection. `delete` removes the document — it is the consumer's erasure primitive and the engine never calls it — as do `eraseConversation`, `eraseOlderThan(cutoff)` and `eraseAll`, while `clear` alone touches only the cache; `docs/session-stores.md` opens with the table that says which is which, because the previous documentation would have led you to believe `delete` erased. Documents carry `createdAt` and `updatedAt`, so a retention period is expressible without per-entry timestamps. **No credential is ever written**: `authHeaders`, `authorization`, `cookie` and `host` are stripped at any depth, not just the top level, and the same `stripCredentialKeys` is exported for stores you write yourself. `mongodb` is an optional peer dependency loaded only when the store actually connects, so a consumer who installs no optional peer can still import the package root and use the in-memory store. **Migration note**: `delete` and `evict` both erase on this store, so the one-line `onDisconnect: sessionId => store.delete(sessionId)` that restores the pre-0.4 close behaviour destroys the conversation on every socket close — point `onDisconnect` at neither. Nothing needs reclaiming by hand: the in-process cache is bounded by `maxAgeMs` (eight hours by default) and drops stale entries without touching a document. This does **not** deliver conversation resume, and it does not make the engine safe to run as more than one instance against one conversation.\n- `withRetry(provider, policy)` decorator wraps any `AIProvider` with per-attempt retry and timeout logic. Pass it a `RetryPolicy` — all fields optional — to absorb transient provider failures before they reach the caller. Defaults: `maxAttempts: 3`, `baseDelayMs: 500`, `maxDelayMs: 5000`, `firstChunkTimeoutMs: 30000`, `idleChunkTimeoutMs: 30000`. A hung request (first chunk never arrives) is abandoned and retried; a stalled stream (idle after the first chunk) surfaces as an `AIError` with code `TIMEOUT` and is not retried, because chunks already streamed cannot be un-sent. Backoff is exponential with full jitter. Both `withRetry` and `RetryPolicy` are exported from the package root.\n- `HalEngineConfig` gains an optional `resilience?: RetryPolicy` field. When present, `createHalEngine` wraps the provider with `withRetry` between `createProvider` and `createChatOrchestrator`; when absent, behaviour is unchanged. This is a strictly additive change — no existing call to `createHalEngine` is affected, so the version bump is MINOR.\n\n### Changed\n\n- Every `SessionStore` member is now `Awaitable<T>` (`T | Promise<T>`) rather than a bare `T`, so a store backed by a database is expressible for the first time. `Awaitable` is exported from the package root. **A synchronous implementation needs no change** — `T` is assignable to `T | Promise<T>` in a return position — and `InMemorySessionStore` is unedited. The WebSocket connection handler now awaits `create` and the release that follows a close: it pauses the socket across the await so a frame sent before `connected` arrives is buffered rather than dropped, attaches its `close` listener before the await so a socket that closes mid-create leaves no session behind, and catches a rejection from either call so one store failure closes one socket instead of ending the process with an unhandled rejection. **Migration note**: the `connected` frame now arrives one tick later, after the awaited `create`, so a client that waits for it (as the protocol already requires) sees no difference; only code that assumed the frame was emitted synchronously with the upgrade does.\n- **The engine no longer erases a conversation when its socket closes.** The WebSocket close handler called `sessionStore.delete(sessionId)` unconditionally, which meant that behind a durable store every conversation was written and then destroyed the moment the customer closed their tab — the opposite of what this engine's own documentation promised. `SessionStore.delete` now has no engine-internal caller at all and is purely your erasure primitive. The credentials the socket carried are cleared from the session on close, since they were issued for a request that is over; the entries are untouched. **Migration note**: to restore the old behaviour exactly, pass `onDisconnect: sessionId => store.delete(sessionId)` — that one line is the whole migration. If you rely on the default store not growing, note that it is now bounded by `maxAgeMs` (eight hours) rather than by the close handler; that is a memory bound, not a retention policy, and the retention decision remains yours.\n- The `onError` hook now fires for every rejection inside a turn, not only those that are an `Error`. A provider that rejects with a string or a plain object used to skip `onError` entirely while still rejecting to the caller, so a failure counter or alert built on `onError` missed those turns. Such a rejection now reaches `onError` as an `Error` whose `message` describes the thrown value and whose `cause` is the original value; an `Error` is passed through as the same instance. The caller still receives the original rejection unchanged. Existing `onError` handlers keep compiling; a thrown string arrives as the `message` unchanged, while a plain object arrives as its listed fields, so read `error.cause` for the raw value.\n- The `afterModelResponse` hook now receives the accumulated usage across every provider call in the turn, not only the last one. Existing consumers that use this hook for billing or audit will see larger totals for turns that called tools — this is the correct number. A turn with no tool calls is unchanged.\n\n### Fixed\n\n- `onError` and `afterSession` hooks now fire when `beforeSession` throws, matching the guarantee the hook names imply. Previously, a throw from `beforeSession` escaped the `try`/`finally` block entirely, so neither hook ran. **Migration note**: if your `afterSession` hook assumed it would only be called after a full session turn, it will now also be called when `beforeSession` itself fails — the hook can inspect the session to detect this case (no entries beyond the initial user message will have been processed).\n\n## [0.3.0] - 2026-09-29\n\n**Upgrading from 0.2.x.** Nothing is removed and no existing signature changes, but check these before you bump:\n\n- `OrchestratorHooks` gains two optional members, `beforeToolCall` and `onToolBudgetExhausted`. Code that checks the hooks exhaustively, for example `satisfies Record<keyof OrchestratorHooks, …>`, fails to type-check until it names both.\n- `ToolRegistry.execute` now validates tool input against each tool's `inputSchema`, so a tool that produced its own message for malformed input receives only valid input; the model reads the registry's validation message instead.\n- `maxToolRounds` now counts executed rounds: the default of 5 runs 5 tool rounds, not 6. Raise it by one to keep the old count.\n- Offering a bare access token as the only WebSocket subprotocol still works but is deprecated: offer `hal.v1` beside it now, because the next minor release stops accepting a bare token.\n- New dependencies: `ajv`, and `cookie` moves from `^0.7` to `^2`.\n\n### Added\n\n- `VertexConfig` gains an optional `apiEndpoint` field, forwarded verbatim to the `@google-cloud/vertexai` SDK constructor. Deployers who must keep inference in the EU multi-region can set `apiEndpoint: 'aiplatform.eu.rep.googleapis.com'`; the change is additive and the default behaviour (endpoint derived from `location`) is unchanged.\n- `HalEngineConfig.transport` now accepts three optional extension points: `additionalRoutes` (mounts a router under `basePath`, unauthenticated), `rootRoutes` (mounts a router at `/`, after the `basePath` router and before the 404 catch-all), and `errorHandler` (replaces Express's default HTML error page). Pass none of them and the app is identical to before. See `docs/getting-started.md` for usage and the GDPR / NIS-2 notes that apply to unauthenticated routes.\n- `OrchestratorHooks` gains an optional `beforeToolCall(session, call)` hook that fires before each known tool's executor, once per call per round. Return `undefined` to let the call run, or a `ToolResponse` to decline it: the executor is skipped and the model reads your `result` as that call's `tool_result`, with `clientMessages` and `suppressAssistantResponse` behaving as a tool's would. A hook that throws declines the call instead of failing the turn. Use it for an audit of what the model attempted or an EU AI Act Article 14 human-oversight control. The hook receives the model's raw tool input and, through `session`, the caller's `authHeaders`, so don't log either. Additive: with no hook installed, nothing changes.\n- `OrchestratorHooks` gains an optional `onToolBudgetExhausted(session, budget)` hook, and `ToolBudgetInfo` (`{maxToolRounds, requestedTools}`) and `TOOL_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` are exported. It fires when the model asks for a tool round `maxToolRounds` refuses, after the last provider call and before `afterModelResponse`. Return a sentence and the turn ends on it as an ordinary assistant entry, so the user sees a degraded answer instead of silence; the engine writes no prose of its own. Every exhausted run now also ends with a `{type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_budget_exhausted'}` chunk, a new value for the existing field rather than a new type, which the WebSocket transport sends nothing for. The turn still ends with `stream_end`, never `error`. Additive: with no hook installed, the wire is unchanged.\n- `AssistantEntry` and `ThinkingEntry` gain an optional `truncated: true`, set on an entry the run could not finish: a provider failure mid-stream, or a round that ends on a tool call with no `stop`. The server re-sends such an entry as an `entry_upsert` carrying the flag just before its `entry_commit`, and the flag stays on the stored entry, so a client can render a cut-off answer as cut off live or on a replay, without relying on the transient `error` frame. An entry committed normally never carries it; a suppressed entry is flagged in the session but never re-sent. Text the thinking-tag parser was still holding back is now kept in a cut-off entry, as it already was on a normal stop. Additive: a client that ignores the field renders what it did before, and the extra `entry_upsert` replaces the entry with the content it already has.\n\n### Changed\n\n- Every `user_message` now ends in exactly one `stream_end`, including a run that fails and a message that fails validation; the `error` frame is advisory rather than terminal. Unparseable JSON remains the one frame answered with `INVALID_FORMAT` and no `stream_end`. **Migration note**: `stream_end` may now follow an `error` frame, so treat `stream_end` (or the socket closing) as the only end of a run. The one live consumer handles the two frame types independently, and both clear its processing state, so it keeps working unchanged and no deprecation period is served by delaying.\n- Every assistant and thinking entry a run opens is now committed when the run ends, however it ends: a provider that throws mid-stream, or a round that ends on a tool call with no `stop`, no longer leaves an entry with `isStreaming: true` in the session. The partial content is kept exactly as streamed, and on a throw its `entry_commit` arrives before the `error` frame. **Migration note**: `entry_commit` now arrives on paths that previously sent none, so a client counting commit frames sees more of them. No wire type changes, and a client that already stops streaming on `stream_end` renders the same result.\n- `maxToolRounds` now bounds the tool rounds that are actually executed: at most `maxToolRounds` rounds run, and the provider is called at most `maxToolRounds + 1` times. Previously the loop ran one extra round whose tool results no provider call ever read, so the default of 5 executed 6 rounds; it now executes 5, and the sixth provider call still reads the fifth round's results. `maxToolRounds: 0` now means one provider call and no tool execution, a negative value behaves as 0, and a non-finite value falls back to the default. When the budget runs out, the engine logs one `warn` (`tool budget exhausted`) naming the tools it did not run. **Migration note**: no exported type or signature changes, but a tool with a side effect in that final, unread round no longer runs there; to restore the old count, raise `maxToolRounds` by one.\n- `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` now carries a JSDoc comment. The field already shipped\n  in `0.2.x`; the comment makes the lifecycle-hook seam discoverable from the published type\n  declarations so editors surface it on hover without a trip to the README.\n- `ToolRegistry.execute` now validates tool input against the declared `inputSchema` before invoking the executor. A schema-invalid call returns a descriptive `ToolResponse` to the model (naming the failing property path and constraint, never the value) rather than throwing, so the model can retry within the existing tool-round budget. Extra properties pass through to the executor unchanged. **Migration note**: an executor that previously hand-validated its own input now receives only input that has already passed the declared schema; a tool that relied on seeing malformed input to produce its own error message will no longer see it.\n- The WebSocket server no longer echoes the access token in its handshake response. A client offers the new `hal.v1` marker beside its token (`new WebSocket(url, ['hal.v1', token])`, or `HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL`, now exported from the package root) and the server answers only `hal.v1`, so the token stays out of the 101 response headers and off the connected socket's `protocol` property. The token is read as the first offered value that is not the marker, in either order, and still reaches tools as `authHeaders.authorization`. `credentialFromSubprotocol`, also newly exported, reads it the same way for your own `WsAuthenticator`. **Migration note**: every client must be updated to offer `hal.v1` alongside its token, and an authenticator that took the first `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` value as the token now gets `hal.v1` from an updated client, so read it with `credentialFromSubprotocol(req.headers['sec-websocket-protocol'])` instead. From the next MINOR, a client that offers only a bare token receives a 101 with no `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` header, and `ws` and Chromium both fail such a handshake; this release still accepts a bare token (see Deprecated), because `0.2.x` documented it.\n\n### Deprecated","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01GQikSYvHHdPRst2RcHo8KA","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015fmJU9wVvYixC7jKq7eBjW","type":"tool_result","content":"name: CI\n\non:\n  pull_request:\n  push:\n    branches: [main]\n  # Manual runs: re-checking a branch against a moved main, or confirming a gate after\n  # changing one, without pushing an empty commit.\n  workflow_dispatch:\n  # An advisory published after a merge is invisible until someone opens a pull request, and this\n  # repository can go days without one. Monday 07:00 UTC; only `build-chain` answers it.\n  schedule:\n    - cron: '0 7 * * 1'\n\n# Every job here only reads the checkout; the repo default is write.\npermissions:\n  contents: read\n\n# A newer push to the same branch supersedes an in-flight run. main is exempt:\n# every commit there should have its own recorded result.\nconcurrency:\n  group: ci-${{ github.ref }}\n  cancel-in-progress: ${{ github.ref != 'refs/heads/main' }}\n\njobs:\n  verify:\n    name: Verify\n    # The scheduled run exists for the dependency audit; re-running the suite on an unchanged\n    # tree every Monday would report nothing new.\n    if: github.event_name != 'schedule'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n        with:\n          persist-credentials: false\n          # Full history, not for this job but for the spec anchor check below:\n          # it resolves cited content from\n          # origin/main, which a shallow clone cannot serve.\n          fetch-depth: 0\n\n      - uses: actions/setup-node@820762786026740c76f36085b0efc47a31fe5020 # v7.0.0\n        with:\n          node-version: '22'\n          cache: npm\n\n      # --ignore-scripts so `prepare` does not build on install: what a lifecycle script\n      # runs is out of this workflow's control, and the Build step below is where a broken\n      # compile should be reported. Type check catches it earlier still.\n      - name: Install\n        run: npm ci --ignore-scripts\n\n      # Every `npm run` step below is one link of the `verify` script in\n      # package.json, in the same order - scripts/verify-script.test.ts holds\n      # the two together - and kept as separate steps so a red run names the\n      # gate that failed rather than a single opaque command.\n      - name: Type check\n        run: npm run typecheck\n\n      # example/ is outside tsconfig.json's rootDir, so nothing checked the one\n      # file the README and the guide are written against.\n      - name: Type check the example\n        run: npm run typecheck:example\n\n      - name: Lint (--max-warnings 0)\n        run: npm run eslint\n\n      # Reports, never blocks. The one rule it runs is `warn`, so the step exits\n      # 0 on its own and carries no continue-on-error: a non-zero exit here now\n      # means the config itself broke, and that should fail. Its findings render\n      # as warning annotations rather than failures, which is what a report on a\n      # passing check should look like.\n      #\n      # require-statement-links counts spec statements with no test behind them.\n      # Most of what it reports needs a test written rather than a line changed,\n      # so the count is meant to be visible on every run and to fall over time.\n      # Its test-side mirror, require-spec-link, is satisfied and gates in the\n      # Lint step above. Never add a rule here to escape a red gate -\n      # eslint.config.backlog.mjs says what belongs.\n      - name: Spec traceability backlog (report)\n        run: npm run lint:backlog\n\n      - name: Format check\n        run: npm run prettier:check\n\n      # Runs the whole suite and fails it on the committed coverage floor in\n      # jest.config.js, so this is the test gate rather than an extra pass.\n      - name: Test (with coverage floor)\n        run: npm run test:coverage\n\n      - name: Build\n        run: npm run build\n\n      # Anchors drift silently: a cited file gaining or losing lines above the\n      # cited one leaves the link pointing at different content, and the spec\n      # still reads as cited. Resolves content at origin/main, which the\n      # fetch-depth: 0 above exists to provide. A red check is fixed by running\n      # `node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs` and committing the result.\n      - name: Spec anchor check\n        run: node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs --check origin/main\n\n      # A citation counts as coverage only in its statement's trailing\n      # parenthetical; one written mid-statement is dropped by the Lore job in silence,\n      # so the spec reads as cited while its coverage is zero. A red check is\n      # fixed by moving the link to the end of its statement, never by deleting\n      # it (AGENTS.md § Spec Test Links).\n      # Every fenced typescript block in the three documented files is generated\n      # from source; hand transcription is what produced the defects this catches.\n      - name: Documented code blocks\n        run: npm run docs:check\n\n      # Three rules that held only because somebody remembered them: a path in prose that resolves,\n      # a spike that says it is a spike, and an example region inside the EU.\n      - name: Documented paths resolve\n        run: npm run docs:check-paths\n\n      - name: Spike documents carry a status block\n        run: npm run docs:check-spikes\n\n      - name: Documented regions\n        run: npm run check:regions\n\n      # A line number is a derived pointer; the name is what the author cited. This catches a citation\n      # whose line moved under it, which no baseline comparison can see on a branch that edits the spec.\n      - name: Spec citations name their test\n        run: npm run spec:names\n\n      - name: Spec link placement\n        run: npm run check:spec-links\n\n      # Lead paragraphs and a spec's status-vs-coverage are ESLint's job; the\n      # one verdict a `files` glob cannot express - that an ADR's frontmatter\n      # status parses, without holding the ADR to the coverage tier - is this\n      # script's. `check:spec-status -- --coverage` lists unlinked statements\n      # and never gates.\n      - name: Spec status\n        run: npm run check:spec-status\n\n      # A release note and a commit subject have different readers, so this is a\n      # file the author writes rather than something derived from the log. The\n      # label is the escape hatch for a src/ change nothing installable observes.\n      - name: Changelog entry\n        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request' && !contains(github.event.pull_request.labels.*.name, 'no-changelog')\n        # No fetch: the checkout above resolves every head into refs/remotes\n        # with fetch-depth 0, and its credential is gone by the time a step\n        # runs, so fetching here asked an unauthenticated git for a private\n        # repository and exited 128 before the gate was ever reached.\n        run: bash scripts/check-changelog.sh \"origin/${{ github.base_ref }}\"\n\n  # Separate job, not a step on `verify`: these two check what the build chain\n  # pulls in rather than what the code does, and a red audit should not read as","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d0b47252-f84a-4d84-a3ea-d6613060a07e","uuid":"0cf9580b-3e86-48fd-8507-00f042a2ed27","timestamp":"2026-11-02T16:24:24.073Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"name: CI\n\non:\n  pull_request:\n  push:\n    branches: [main]\n  # Manual runs: re-checking a branch against a moved main, or confirming a gate after\n  # changing one, without pushing an empty commit.\n  workflow_dispatch:\n  # An advisory published after a merge is invisible until someone opens a pull request, and this\n  # repository can go days without one. Never add a rule here to escape a red gate -\n      # eslint.config.backlog.mjs says what belongs.\n      - name: Spec traceability backlog (report)\n        run: npm run lint:backlog\n\n      - name: Format check\n        run: npm run prettier:check\n\n      # Runs the whole suite and fails it on the committed coverage floor in\n      # jest.config.js, so this is the test gate rather than an extra pass.\n      - name: Test (with coverage floor)\n        run: npm run test:coverage\n\n      - name: Build\n        run: npm run build\n\n      # Anchors drift silently: a cited file gaining or losing lines above the\n      # cited one leaves the link pointing at different content, and the spec\n      # still reads as cited. Resolves content at origin/main, which the\n      # fetch-depth: 0 above exists to provide. A red check is fixed by running\n      # `node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs` and committing the result.\n      - name: Spec anchor check\n        run: node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs --check origin/main\n\n      # A citation counts as coverage only in its statement's trailing\n      # parenthetical; one written mid-statement is dropped by the Lore job in silence,\n      # so the spec reads as cited while its coverage is zero. A red check is\n      # fixed by moving the link to the end of its statement, never by deleting\n      # it (AGENTS.md § Spec Test Links).\n      # Every fenced typescript block in the three documented files is generated\n      # from source; hand transcription is what produced the defects this catches.\n      - name: Documented code blocks\n        run: npm run docs:check\n\n      # Three rules that held only because somebody remembered them: a path in prose that resolves,\n      # a spike that says it is a spike, and an example region inside the EU.\n      - name: Documented paths resolve\n        run: npm run docs:check-paths\n\n      - name: Spike documents carry a status block\n        run: npm run docs:check-spikes\n\n      - name: Documented regions\n        run: npm run check:regions\n\n      # A line number is a derived pointer; the name is what the author cited. This catches a citation\n      # whose line moved under it, which no baseline comparison can see on a branch that edits the spec.\n      - name: Spec citations name their test\n        run: npm run spec:names\n\n      - name: Spec link placement\n        run: npm run check:spec-links\n\n      # Lead paragraphs and a spec's status-vs-coverage are ESLint's job; the\n      # one verdict a `files` glob cannot express - that an ADR's frontmatter\n      # status parses, without holding the ADR to the coverage tier - is this\n      # script's. `check:spec-status -- --coverage` lists unlinked statements\n      # and never gates.\n      - name: Spec status\n        run: npm run check:spec-status\n\n      # A release note and a commit subject have different readers, so this is a\n      # file the author writes rather than something derived from the log. The\n      # label is the escape hatch for a src/ change nothing installable observes.\n      - name: Changelog entry\n        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request' && !contains(github.event.pull_request.labels.*.name, 'no-changelog')\n        # No fetch: the checkout above resolves every head into refs/remotes\n        # with fetch-depth 0, and its credential is gone by the time a step\n        # runs, so fetching here asked an unauthenticated git for a private\n        # repository and exited 128 before the gate was ever reached.\n        run: bash scripts/check-changelog.sh \"origin/${{ github.base_ref }}\"\n\n  # Separate job, not a step on `verify`: these two check what the build chain\n  # pulls in rather than what the code does, and a red audit should not read as","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_015fmJU9wVvYixC7jKq7eBjW","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

`afterSession` ([validated by: error path calls onError then afterSession](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L401)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:216:- `beforeSession` runs before any other hook ([validated by: called before anything else](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L106)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:217:- `beforeUserInput` receives the content of the last user message ([validated by: receives the last user message content](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L174)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:218:- `beforeUserInput` can modify that message by returning a different string ([validated by: modifies user message when returning different value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L189)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:219:- `afterUserInput` receives the message as `beforeUserInput` left it, not as the client sent it ([validated by: receives user message after any beforeUserInput modification](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L204)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:220:- `beforeModelResponse` can replace the system prompt, for example loading it from a `PromptStore` ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:221:- `afterModelResponse` receives the collected response text and the usage the provider reported ([validated by: receives collected response text and usage](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L236)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:222:- `afterModelResponse` does not run when the stream throws, so it never reports a response that was not delivered ([validated by: not called when stream throws](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L256)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:223:- `onError` receives the session and the error ([validated by: called with session and error when stream fails](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L278)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:224:- `onError` observes rather than handles: the error still propagates to the caller after it returns ([validated by: error still propagates after onError hook](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L300)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:226:- `afterSession` runs after everything else completes ([validated by: called after everything completes](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L145)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:227:- `afterSession` fires even on error ([validated by: called even when an error occurs](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L155)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:333:5. If the tool returned `clientMessages`, they are forwarded to the frontend as-is, in one `tool_result` chunk ([validated by: forwards the client messages a tool returned as one tool_result chunk](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L511))\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:334:6. If the tool set `suppressAssistantResponse`, the AI's next reply is kept in session context but hidden from the frontend ([validated by: asks for suppression when the tool says the reply is already handled](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L541))\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:335:7. The orchestrator re-queries the AI provider with the updated messages, and the chunks of every round reach the client in order ([validated by: runs another round after a tool call and streams both rounds in order](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L476))\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:336:8. This repeats until the budget is spent: at most `maxToolRounds` tool rounds are executed, and the provider is called at most `maxToolRounds + 1` times, so the default of 5 executes five rounds and makes six model calls, the last of which reads the fifth round's results. A round requested after that is not executed; see [the tool budget spec](../hal-engi\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:347:- The loop ends as soon as a round stops with `end_turn`, and nothing further is asked of the provider ([validated by: stops after one round when nothing asked for a tool](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L488)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:348:- An `entry_upsert` a tool returns is appended to the session and its index rewritten to the position it actually landed in, because a tool cannot know how long the session already is ([validated by: appends an upserted entry to the session and rewrites its index to match](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L524)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:349:- The response text a hook sees is the text of every round joined, not only the last ([validated by: joins the text of every round, not only the last](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L553)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:350:- Usage an earlier round reported is kept when a later round reports none, so a tool round does not erase the token count ([validated by: keeps the usage an earlier round reported when a later round reports none](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L585)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:351:- The usage `afterModelResponse` receives is `totalUsage`: the sum of what every provider call in the turn reported, so a turn that ran tools counts every round rather than the last one alone ([validated by: sums the usage of every round, not only the last](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L603)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:352:- A round that reports no usage drops out of the sum rather than voiding it: the rounds that did report are still counted, and a turn where nothing reported reports nothing ([validated by: counts every reporting round even when a round between them reports none](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L625)).\nspecs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md:15:- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.\nspecs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md:17:- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no mode\nspecs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md:18:- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and is passed `totalUsage` — the sum of the usage every provider call in the turn reported, not the last round alone ([validated by: sums the usage of every round, not only the last](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L603)).\nspecs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md:25:- A validation failure becomes a tool result (not a thrown error), so the orchestrator delivers the rejection text to the model and the model can attempt a corrected call; `DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` is the bound that stops a retry cycle from spinning ([validated by: resolves processMessage and makes the validation-rejection text visible to the p\n----\nscripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs\n#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Re-points specs' [validated by](../../src/X.test.ts#Lnn) anchors after\n// edits to the cited file - any cited\n// repository file, whatever its kind: each anchor's line number is\n// resolved to the content it cited in the base ref's copy of that file,\n// that content is found in the working copy (compared with quotes and\n// whitespace dropped, so a formatter pass is not drift), and the anchor is\n// rewritten to the match whose surrounding lines agree with the baseline's. When the cited\n// content occurs on several working lines, each candidate is scored by how\n// many of the baseline's neighbouring lines it reproduces at the same offsets;\n// a candidate whose context uniquely wins is chosen, and a tie is reported as\n// ambiguous for manual fix (exit 1 in both modes) instead of guessing. An\n// anchor into a file built of repeated blocks - a workflow whose jobs share an\n// identical setup - must therefore cite a line unique to its own step, because\n// the resolver refuses to guess between indistinguishable candidates.\n//\n// Usage:\n//   node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs [--check] [base-ref]\n//\n// base-ref defaults to origin/main and must be the baseline the anchors were\n// last correct against. Baseline line numbers are read from the base ref's\n// copy of each spec, never from the working copy, so re-running against the\n// same baseline is a no-op instead of a second translation. A spec whose\n// anchor set (the ordered list of test paths it cites) differs from the base\n// ref is skipped whole: its anchors were authored against the working tree,\n// and no baseline exists to repoint them from. An individual anchor whose\n// line number differs from the base spec's at the same position was\n// deliberately retargeted by a spec edit: it is accepted as authored, never\n// rewritten, and reported as \"retargeted (not checked)\".\n//\n// Independent of any baseline, every anchor must land on a line that exists\n// and carries content: an anchor whose target file is missing, whose line is\n// beyond the end of the file, or whose line is blank or closing punctuation\n// is reported as rotten and fails the run in both modes. This repeats until the budget is spent: at most `maxToolRounds` tool rounds are executed, and the provider is called at most `maxToolRounds + 1` times, so the default of 5 executes five rounds and makes six model calls, the last of which reads the fifth round's results. A round requested after that is not executed; see [the tool budget spec](../hal-engi\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:347:- The loop ends as soon as a round stops with `end_turn`, and nothing further is asked of the provider ([validated by: stops after one round when nothing asked for a tool](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L488)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:348:- An `entry_upsert` a tool returns is appended to the session and its index rewritten to the position it actually landed in, because a tool cannot know how long the session already is ([validated by: appends an upserted entry to the session and rewrites its index to match](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L524)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:349:- The response text a hook sees is the text of every round joined, not only the last ([validated by: joins the text of every round, not only the last](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L553)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:350:- Usage an earlier round reported is kept when a later round reports none, so a tool round does not erase the token count ([validated by: keeps the usage an earlier round reported when a later round reports none](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L585)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:351:- The usage `afterModelResponse` receives is `totalUsage`: the sum of what every provider call in the turn reported, so a turn that ran tools counts every round rather than the last one alone ([validated by: sums the usage of every round, not only the last](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L603)).\nspecs/hal-engine-architecture/spec.md:352:- A round that reports no usage drops out of the sum rather than voiding it: the rounds that did report are still counted, and a turn where nothing reported reports nothing ([validated by: counts every reporting round even when a round between them reports none](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L625)).\nspecs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md:15:- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.\nspecs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md:17:- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no mode\nspecs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md:18:- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and is passed `totalUsage` — the sum of the usage every provider call in the turn reported, not the last round alone ([validated by: sums the usage of every round, not only the last](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L603)).\nspecs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md:25:- A validation failure becomes a tool result (not a thrown error), so the orchestrator delivers the rejection text to the model and the model can attempt a corrected call; `DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` is the bound that stops a retry cycle from spinning ([validated by: resolves processMessage and makes the validation-rejection text visible to the p\n----\nscripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs\n#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Re-points specs' [validated by](../../src/X.test.ts#Lnn) anchors after\n// edits to the cited file - any cited\n// repository file, whatever its kind: each anchor's line number is\n// resolved to the content it cited in the base ref's copy of that file,\n// that content is found in the working copy (compared with quotes and\n// whitespace dropped, so a formatter pass is not drift), and the anchor is\n// rewritten to the match whose surrounding lines agree with the baseline's. When the cited\n// content occurs on several working lines, each candidate is scored by how\n// many of the baseline's neighbouring lines it reproduces at the same offsets;\n// a candidate whose context uniquely wins is chosen, and a tie is reported as\n// ambiguous for manual fix (exit 1 in both modes) instead of guessing. An\n// anchor into a file built of repeated blocks - a workflow whose jobs share an\n// identical setup - must therefore cite a line unique to its own step, because\n// the resolver refuses to guess between indistinguishable candidates.\n//\n// Usage:\n//   node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs [--check] [base-ref]\n//\n// base-ref defaults to origin/main and must be the baseline the anchors were\n// last correct against. Baseline line numbers are read from the base ref's\n// copy of each spec, never from the working copy, so re-running against the\n// same baseline is a no-op instead of a second translation. A spec whose\n// anchor set (the ordered list of test paths it cites) differs from the base\n// ref is skipped whole: its anchors were authored against the working tree,\n// and no baseline exists to repoint them from. An individual anchor whose\n// line number differs from the base spec's at the same position was\n// deliberately retargeted by a spec edit: it is accepted as authored, never\n// rewritten, and reported as \"retargeted (not checked)\".\n//\n// Independent of any baseline, every anchor must land on a line that exists\n// and carries content: an anchor whose target file is missing, whose line is\n// beyond the end of the file, or whose line is blank or closing punctuation\n// is reported as rotten and fails the run in both modes. An anchor\n// into a test file must additionally land on the it/test/describe declaration\n// itself, because a line inside a test body names no case a reader can check.\n// Non-test targets - a workflow, a config, package.json - are exempt from that\n// last check: they have no declaration to land on.\n//\n// Also independent of any baseline, a short-form [Lnnn](...#Lmmm) label must","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01TES9T95eubtv5bgrHxz4YB","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

String(reason);\n-  } catch {\n-    return String(reason);\n+  if (typeof reason === 'object' && reason !== null) {\n+    const fields = Object.entries(reason).map(([key, value]) => `${key}: ${String(value)}`);\n+    if (fields.length > 0) return `{${fields.join(', ')}}`;\n   }\n+  return String(reason);\n }\n \n // A store outage is not a reason to fail the turn the customer already received.\ncommit 92b378b4d137510fd472db43ac12a6ce14b317b1\nAuthor: loop-bench <loop-bench@localhost>\nDate:   Mon Nov 2 17:13:38 2026 +0100\n\n    fix(orchestrator): repoint spec links and changelog for onError\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Haiku 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\n\ndiff --git a/CHANGELOG.md b/CHANGELOG.md\nindex eab0af2..3c8ebcd 100644\n--- a/CHANGELOG.md\n+++ b/CHANGELOG.md\n@@ -20,6 +20,7 @@ package, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n \n - Every `SessionStore` member is now `Awaitable<T>` (`T | Promise<T>`) rather than a bare `T`, so a store backed by a database is expressible for the first time. `Awaitable` is exported from the pack\n - **The engine no longer erases a conversation when its socket closes.** The WebSocket close handler called `sessionStore.delete(sessionId)` unconditionally, which meant that behind a durable store e\n+- The `onError` hook now fires for every rejection inside a turn, not only those that are an `Error`. This issue does not change it; that is a separate, out-of-scope change owned by no issue in this epic.\n-\n-- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L150](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L150)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01V2ha1YzJb3XX1R9KpDWoiC","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}
